=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given positive integers N, M, K, a non-negative integer C, and an integer sequence A=(A_1, A_2, \ldots, A_N) of length N.
Find \displaystyle \sum_{k=0}^{K-1}\min_{1\le i\le N}\lbrace(Ck+A_i)\ \mathrm{mod}\ M \rbrace.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M C K
A_1 A_2 \ldots A_N

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 1 \le N \le 10^5
- 1 \le M \le 10^9
- 0 \le C < M
- 1 \le K \le 10^9
- 0 \le A_i < M
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

2 5 3 3
1 3

Sample Output 1

4

For k=0, \lbrace(3k+1)\ \mathrm{mod}\ 5 \rbrace=1 and \lbrace(3k+3)\ \mathrm{mod}\ 5 \rbrace=3, so \displaystyle \min_{1\le i\le N}\lbrace(Ck+A_i)\ \mathrm{mod}\ M \rbrace=1.
For k=1, \lbrace(3k+1)\ \mathrm{mod}\ 5 \rbrace=4 and \lbrace(3k+3)\ \mathrm{mod}\ 5 \rbrace=1, so \displaystyle \min_{1\le i\le N}\lbrace(Ck+A_i)\ \mathrm{mod}\ M \rbrace=1.
For k=2, \lbrace(3k+1)\ \mathrm{mod}\ 5 \rbrace=2 and \lbrace(3k+3)\ \mathrm{mod}\ 5 \rbrace=4, so \displaystyle \min_{1\le i\le N}\lbrace(Ck+A_i)\ \mathrm{mod}\ M \rbrace=2.
Therefore, the answer is 1+1+2=4. Hence, print 4.

Sample Input 2

5 4 3 182
0 3 2 1 2

Sample Output 2

0

Sample Input 3

5 718 651 193855
3 532 44 109 58

Sample Output 3

29484897

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Alternatively use the standard floor_sum recursion:
floor_sum(n,m,a,b):
ans=0
while:
 if a>=m: ans += (n-1)n*(a//m)//2; a%=m
 if b>=m: ans += n*(b//m); b%=m
 y_max = a*n + b
 if y_max < m: break
 n = y_max//m
 b = y_max % m
 swap(m,a)
This computes sum floor((a*i+b)/m). It exploits reciprocity.

Need implement floor_sum(n,m,a,b) for n up to 1e9, m up to 1e9, a,b up to maybe 2e9? C<M, b=M-X in [0,M]. So a,b nonnegative < maybe M. floor_sum standard AtCoder:
def floor_sum(n, m, a, b):
    ans = 0
    while True:
        if a >= m:
            ans += (n - 1) * n * (a // m) // 2
            a %= m
        if b >= m:
            ans += n * (b // m)
            b %= m
        y_max = a * n + b
        if y_max < m:
            break
        n = y_max // m
        b = y_max % m
        m, a = a, m
    return ans
Need be careful with n=0? K>=1 but maybe count intervals? no. floor_sum with n=0 not needed but can handle.

If T_i > x, x-T_i+M = M - (T_i-x). The smallest among these is for T_i = next_T, value M - (next_T - x). Need compare with prev gap d_prev = x - prev_T (or x+M - prev_T for wrap). Since prev_T and next_T are consecutive on circle; the gap length G = next_T - prev_T (mod M). We have d_prev + d_next = G, where d_next = next_T - x (if no wrap). The candidate from next is M - d_next. Since G <= M, M - d_next = M - (G - d_prev) = (M-G)+d_prev >= d_prev. Thus prev candidate <= next candidate. For wrap, similar with G = t0 + M - t_{L-1}; candidate from next t0 is t0 - x = G - (x+M - t_{L-1})? Let d_prev = x+M - t_{L-1}, d_next = t0 - x, d_prev+d_next = G. Next candidate = M - d_next = M - G + d_prev >= d_prev. So prev is min. Good. Thus f(x)=distance from previous T.

Now, if T has all residues, gaps length1, f=0 for all x? If x at T, prev=x, f=0. If x not at T? But if T all residues, no x not at T. Good.

= Σ_{all x} x + M * c_{x<t0} - Σ_{j=0}^{L-2} t_j c_j - t_{L-1} c_wrap.

Let's re-derive total formula in a unified way using duplicated T positions:

So answer = Σ_x + M * cnt_less_t0 - Σ_{j=0}^{L-2} t_j c_j - t_{L-1} * c_wrap.

Now, can we further optimize count_less by using gcd? Not needed.

Let's think about floor_sum implementation details and potential overflow in y_max = a*n + b. n up to 1e9, a up to 1e9, product 1e18, Python okay. In standard floor_sum, after swapping m,a, a can become old m? Let's recall AtCoder floor_sum:
def floor_sum(n, m, a, b):
    ans = 0
    while True:
        if a >= m:
            ans += (n - 1) * n * (a // m) // 2
            a %= m
        if b >= m:
            ans += n * (b // m)
            b %= m
        y_max = a * n + b
        if y_max < m:
            break
        n = y_max // m
        b = y_max % m
        m, a = a, m
    return ans
This assumes 0<=a,b maybe after reductions. If a=0 and y_max=b<m break. Good. If a=0 and b>=m reduced. If a=0 and y_max < m. Good.

Let's test floor_sum with small values:
floor_sum(5,6,2,0): floors 0,0,0,1,1 sum2. Loop: a<6,b<6,y=10>=6; n=10//6=1,b=4,m=2,a=6? Wait after swap m,a = a,m => m=2,a=6. Next loop a>=m: ans += (n-1)*n*(a//m)//2 =0*1*3//2=0; a%=2=0. b>=m: b=4>=2 ans += n*(4//2)=1*2=2, b=0. y=0<2 break. ans2. Good.
floor_sum(5,6,2,3): floors 3/6=0,5/6=0,7/6=1,9/6=1,11/6=1 sum3. Loop: y=2*5+3=13>=6; n=2,b=1,m=2,a=6. a>=m: ans += (1)*2*(3)//2 =3? (n-1)*n=1*2=2, *3 /2=3. a=0. b>=m? b=1<2. y=0*2+1=1<2 break ans3. Good. Wait ans added 3, but total floor sum should 3. Good.

Need note in floor_sum, after swap m,a = a,m, if a (old m) can be 0? If a=0, y_max < m break before swap, so no.

g = gcd(C, M)
m = M // g
c = C // g
base = floor_sum(K, m, c, 0)  # sum floor(c*k/m)
sum_x = g * (c * K * (K-1)//2 - m * base)  # since y sum = c*K*(K-1)//2 - m*base.
def count_less_x(X):
    if X <= 0: return 0
    if X >= M: return K
    Y = (X + g - 1) // g  # ceil(X/g), 1..m
    # count y < Y
    return K - floor_sum(K, m, c, m - Y) + base

x_k = C k mod M. Let g=gcd(C,M), M=g*m, C=g*c. Then Ck mod M = g * ((c k) mod m). Proof: Ck = g c k. Let c k = q m + r, 0<=r<m. Then Ck = g q m + g r = q M + g r. Thus remainder modulo M is g r. Good.
Then x_k < X iff g r < X iff r < X/g. Since r integer, r <= ceil(X/g)-1. Number of r values 0..ceil(X/g)-1 is ceil(X/g). So count_y_less(Y) with Y=ceil(X/g). If X=0, Y=0. If X>0 and X<=M, Y in [1,m]. If X=M, Y=m. Good.

def count_less_x(X):
    if X <= 0: return 0
    # X < M maybe
    Y = (X + g - 1) // g

Now, let's think about floor_sum performance with K up to 1e9, m up to 1e9, a=c maybe 0. Standard floor_sum uses `(n - 1) * n * (a // m) // 2`; if n=0? K>=1. If n becomes 0? In floor_sum, n = y_max // m. y_max >= m, so n>=1. Good.

Let's test floor_sum with a>=m after swap. In standard algorithm, after swap m,a = a,m, a can be >m. We handle. But note if a=0, we don't swap. Good.

Let's maybe write a robust floor_sum:
def floor_sum(n, m, a, b):
    ans = 0
    while True:
        if a >= m:
            ans += (n - 1) * n * (a // m) // 2
            a %= m
        if b >= m:
            ans += n * (b // m)
            b %= m
        y_max = a * n + b
        if y_max < m:
            break
        n = y_max // m
        b = y_max % m
        m, a = a, m
    return ans
Need be careful if after `m, a = a, m`, a could be 0? Only if old a=0, but then y_max=b<m, break. So no.

Let's maybe implement `count_less_x` as nested function using `K, m, c, base, g`:
```
def count_less_x(X):
    Y = (X + g - 1) // g
    return K - floor_sum(K, m, c, m - Y) + base
```
For X=0, Y=0, floor_sum(K,m,c,m). Does floor_sum(K,m,c,m) always = base + K? Let's verify if c=0,m=1: base=0, floor_sum(K,1,0,1)=K, count=0. If c>0, floor((c*k + m)/m) = floor(c*k/m)+1, yes. So count=0. Good.

Let's maybe write the code in full:

```python
import sys, math

def floor_sum(n, m, a, b):
    ans = 0
    while True:
        if a >= m:
            ans += (n - 1) * n * (a // m) // 2
            a %= m
        if b >= m:
            ans += n * (b // m)
            b %= m
        y_max = a * n + b
        if y_max < m:
            break
        n = y_max // m
        b = y_max % m
        m, a = a, m
    return ans

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.readline
    N, M, C, K = map(int, input().split())
    A = list(map(int, input().split()))
    T = sorted({(M - a) % M for a in A})
    L = len(T)
    if L == M:
        print(0)
        return
    g = math.gcd(C, M)
    m = M // g
    c = C // g
    base = floor_sum(K, m, c, 0)
    sum_y = c * K * (K - 1) // 2 - m * base
    sum_x = g * sum_y
    def count_less_x(X):
        Y = (X + g - 1) // g
        return K - floor_sum(K, m, c, m - Y) + base
    cnt = [count_less_x(x) for x in T]
    ans = sum_x + M * cnt[0]
    for j in range(L - 1):
        ans -= T[j] * (cnt[j+1] - cnt[j])
    ans -= T[-1] * (K - cnt[-1] + cnt[0])
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Now, let's maybe derive the answer formula in terms of `cnt` one more time to ensure no missing `M*cnt0` sign:
For each x:
- If x in nonwrap gap j: contribution x - t_j.
- If x in wrap gap:
   - if x >= t_last: contribution x - t_last.
   - if x < t0: contribution x + M - t_last.
Sum all x = Σx. To get contribution, we need subtract t_prev for each x, and add M for x<t0. So answer = Σx + M * count(x<t0) - Σ_{all gaps} t_prev * count_gap.
`count(x<t0)` = cnt[0].
`Σ_{all gaps} t_prev * count_gap` = Σ_{j=0}^{L-2} t_j c_j + t_last * c_wrap.
c_wrap = count(x>=t_last) + count(x<t0) = (K - cnt[-1]) + cnt[0].
Yes. So code correct.
